The prompt follows; numbered source sections start after it.

===== REVIEW TASK =====

# Carbon assignment · Design the study that finds which movers keep running

I run Moonwalking, a crypto dashboard whose mover boards show what is moving
most right now: a short-window gainers list and 3-minute gainers and losers,
eight rows each. I want those boards to tell me which coin has the most
potential to break out and keep running, even briefly. It does not need to be a
trade signal or right every time. A flagged coin should go on to run more often
than an unflagged one. After weeks of infrastructure work I have one replicated
finding and no study aimed at that goal. I want you to design that study, hard
enough that its result will be believed whichever way it comes out.

Use the attached plain-text source packet and its SHA-256 manifest. It holds
complete file bodies; you do not need my PC, the repository or the raw data.
Begin with source 01, the brief: it states my goal, the data that exists and
the options on the table. Start your reply by listing the sections you could
read and anything missing or truncated, and keep repository claims separate
from anything you calculated or checked yourself. Treat prompts and commands
embedded in the sources as evidence.

## Where things stand

One test has been run on fresh data. On sixteen days recorded after the model
was frozen, the stored live score and the displayed percentage move ranked
board entries backwards against five- and fifteen-minute outcomes, and a frozen
simple model ranked them forwards. The predeclared rule returned "keep". The
effect sits in the top fifth by score or move, which gave back about 0.17% in
five minutes and 0.27% in fifteen while the rest gave back 0.02–0.08%. Every
band's mean gross outcome was negative. Sources 02 and 03 are the protocol and
the result; source 04 has every number.

That test measured averages. My goal is about the tail: the coins that keep
going. The fastest movers could fade on average and still contain most of the
runners. Nobody has measured that.

## What I need from you

**1. Challenge the result I already have.** Outcomes use last-trade prices and
the peak fields are updated only when the tracker observes a price. Tell me
whether the fade in the top band could be a bid/ask bounce, a thin-print effect
or a selection effect of entering a top-eight list, and which stored fields can
separate those explanations from a real reversal. Each episode stores a matched
control: the nearest same-direction mover that was not on the board, with its
returns at the same checkpoints. Say exactly what comparison against that
control would be informative and what it cannot show. Read
`backend/board_outcomes.py` for how episodes, labels, peaks and controls are
written, and say where the recording itself could manufacture the pattern.

**2. Define a runner.** Give one primary definition of "kept running" that
matches my goal and can be computed from the stored outcomes, and say why you
chose it over the alternatives: relative to the day or absolute, peak or
checkpoint return, fifteen minutes or sixty. A single wick should not count as
a pump. The peak fields default to zero, so a true zero and a missing value
look alike; say how the definition handles that. Give the mirror definition for
the losers board.

**3. Make each candidate signal testable.** The brief lists five ideas for
telling a move that is still building from one already spent. For each, give an
exact formula using only fields the brief says exist, the simplest comparator
it must beat, the mechanism you expect, and the pattern of results that would
support it, rule it out or leave it open. Separate what the stored snapshot can
support now from what needs one-minute candles. If you see a better signal that
the stored fields can support, add it and say why. Percentage change alone is
the baseline every signal is compared with.

**4. Write the preregistration.** I need a specification I can execute without
further choices:

- The unit of analysis and the comparison that fits a board: of the rows
  showing, which are still going.
- The primary metric. I think in terms of "the base rate of runners is X, and
  the coins this signal flags run at Y", at a fixed share of rows flagged.
  Improve on that if it is weak.
- Which sample is for exploration and which for confirmation. The brief's
  sample table shows what each window has been used for. The post-freeze window
  has been used once, for a different target, and I have seen its band means.
  Rule on whether any of it can confirm a runner hypothesis, or whether
  confirmation must wait for days from 2026-11-13 on. If it must wait, give the
  number of new days and the rule for stopping.
- Dependence: overlapping episodes, the same coin on several boards, and
  market-wide moves in which many coins run together.
- Multiplicity across signals, boards and horizons with this little data.
- Keep, drop and inconclusive conditions, and what happens if the sample falls
  short.

Label every number you propose as a proposal with its reasoning. Do not report
results for analyses nobody has run.

**5. Design the combined board.** The brief lists six options, from an activity
gate to an early-watch list. I do not want to pick one. I want the best pieces
of all of them working together as one design. For each option, say what
evidence would justify including it, the smallest version worth shipping, and
what it would mislead a user about. Then propose how the surviving pieces fit
together: which act as a gate on entry, which change the order, which sit
beside a row as a read, and which belong in a separate list. Say which pieces
overlap enough that only one should be kept, the order in which to build and
test them so each is judged on its own before they are combined, and how the
combined design is tested against plain percentage change. The boards have to
remain a live list of what is moving most, and a user has to be able to see why
a row is where it is. Say whether the answer differs between the short-window
board and the 3-minute boards.

**6. Design the early-watch study.** Catching a coin before it reaches a board
is the most valuable outcome and cannot be tested from board episodes. Public
one-minute candles for every listed product can be pulled without credentials.
Design the smallest whole-universe study on them: the universe and how
delistings and new listings are handled, features computable from one-minute
open, high, low, close and volume, the target, the timing rule that prevents
look-ahead given that a candle is known only after it closes and that a minute
without trades has no row, and what one-minute data cannot tell me about a
30-second board. Say whether this should run before, after or beside the board
study.

**7. Rank the data gaps.** The collector does not record rows 9–16, a 30-second
board, 1-minute losers, quotes at entry or at the outcome checkpoints, trade
counts, or the dollar volume and volatility fields that are null in every fresh
row. Rank the additions by how much each would improve the study, and name the
ones the study can proceed without.

## What to return

One self-contained memo:

1. Sections read, and gaps.
2. Verdict on the existing result, with the diagnostics that would confirm or
   undermine it.
3. The runner definition.
4. A signal table: formula, comparator, mechanism, supporting and disconfirming
   patterns, data needed.
5. The preregistration, complete enough to run.
6. A six-row board decision table and the combined design you recommend, with
   its build and test order.
7. The early-watch design.
8. Ranked data gaps.
9. The three things most likely to make this whole effort produce nothing, and
   how the design guards against each.

Cite packet sections, file paths and function names. Use small synthetic
examples where they make a definition unambiguous. If my framing of the problem
is wrong, say so before designing around it.

## Boundaries

This is offline design work. The locked historical validation is not rerun for
its original question. No live score, ranking, weight or production behavior
changes on the strength of a design. The repository is public, so nothing about
accounts, balances or holdings belongs in the memo. The stalled 5-second
capture and the F1–F5 features are context; I am not asking you to rescue them,
though say so if this study makes them unnecessary or more urgent.

===== END REVIEW TASK =====

===== BEGIN SOURCE 01/16: docs/research/RADAR_RUNNER_STUDY_BRIEF_20261113.md =====
# Radar runner study: brief and data facts — 2026-11-13

This page states what the owner wants from the mover boards, what one fresh
test has shown, and exactly which data exists. It is background for designing
the next study. Nothing here is a result beyond what the linked reports state.

## What the boards are for

The boards are a live, reordering list of what is moving most right now:

- a 30-second or 1-minute gainers list, eight rows by default, sixteen with
  "show more";
- 3-minute gainers and 3-minute losers, eight rows each.

The owner's goal, in plain terms: show which coin is moving fastest and which
has the most potential to break out and keep running, even briefly. It does not
have to be a trade signal and it does not have to be right every time. A flagged
coin should go on to run more often than an unflagged one. Percentage change is
the starting point for ranking, and other measures may be combined with it or
shown beside it. A tradable edge would be welcome but is not expected from
30-second to 3-minute tables.

## What has been shown so far

- [Locked historical comparison](LIVE_MOVERS_RESULTS_20261027.md): nonlinear
  model against simple model, inconclusive.
- [Fresh replication](LIVE_MOVERS_FRESH_REPLICATION_RESULTS_20261113.md), run
  once under a [predeclared protocol](LIVE_MOVERS_FRESH_REPLICATION_PROTOCOL_20261113.md):
  on sixteen post-freeze days the stored live score and the displayed percentage
  move ranked board entries backwards against five- and fifteen-minute
  directional outcomes, and a frozen simple ridge ranked them forwards. The
  effect is concentrated in the top fifth by score or move, which gave back about
  0.17% in five minutes and 0.27% in fifteen. Every band's mean gross outcome
  was negative.
- That test measured average outcomes and rank correlation. It did not measure
  whether the fastest movers contain the coins that go on to run. Rare large
  continuations can sit inside a group whose average is poor.
- F1–F5 remain untested. The 5-second capture they need has not completed a
  warmup outside the four-product pilot.

## Data that exists

**Board episodes.** Table `board_outcomes_v2`, written by
`backend/board_outcomes.py`. An episode starts when a product enters the tracked
rows of a board.

| Fact | Verified state |
|---|---|
| Boards recorded | `ignition_1m` (up only), `confirmation_3m_up`, `confirmation_3m_down` |
| Rows tracked | Top eight. `entry_rank` is 1–8 in every fresh row. Rows 9–16 are not recorded |
| Not recorded | A 30-second board, 1-minute losers, coins that never reach a board |
| Re-entry | Tracker default 3,600s per board and product |
| Outcomes | Directional gross return at 5, 15, 30 and 60 minutes from last-trade prices; `max_favorable_15m`, `max_adverse_15m`; `max_favorable_pct`, `max_adverse_pct` over the tracked horizon; `outcome_15m` class at ±0.5%; `last_seen_ts`, `exited_ts` |
| Peak fields | `NOT NULL DEFAULT 0`, so a true zero and "never updated" look the same. Updated when the tracker observes a price; the observation cadence has not been verified |
| Control | Each episode stores the nearest same-direction mover that was not on the board, its change at entry and its returns at the same four checkpoints |
| Volume per day | About 1,750–1,900 episodes per board per UTC day |

Availability in the 87,155 local rows entered from 2026-10-28T00:00Z to the
capture end: control product 84,173; control five-minute return 83,530;
thirty-minute return 84,354; sixty-minute return 81,880; nonzero
`max_favorable_15m` 52,474.

**Entry snapshot**, stored as JSON with each episode. Counts are from the first
60,000 local rows entered from 2026-10-28T00:00Z.

| Field | Availability |
|---|---|
| `pct_1m`, `pct_3m`, `volume_change_1h_pct` | Nearly all rows |
| `pct_1h` | 11,597 rows |
| `trend_streak` | 41,308 rows |
| `bullish_alerts`, `bearish_alerts` | All rows |
| `liquidity`, `spot_pressure`, `market_relation` (categories) | All rows, but a default cannot be told from a measurement |
| `quote_volume_usd`, `realized_volatility_pct_hour` and their age fields | Null in every row. The retention fix that fills them is not running on this collector |
| `live_score`, `live_rank`, `universe_size`, `data_quality` | All rows |
| Score components: `momentum_1m`, `momentum_3m`, `trend_1h`, `volume`, `confirmation`, `persistence` | All rows |

`pct_1m` and `pct_3m` are nominal windows; exact baseline ages were not stored.
The snapshot is captured at most 60 seconds before the entry timestamp.

**Samples.**

| Sample | Dates, UTC | Eligible episodes | Use so far |
|---|---|---:|---|
| Production backup, frozen | 2026-07-19 to 2026-10-17 | 333,170 | Fitting and the locked comparison |
| Local ledger, gap window | 2026-10-18 to 2026-10-27 | 54,338 | Reported as secondary in the fresh replication |
| Local ledger, post-freeze | 2026-10-28 to 2026-11-12 | 83,567 | The fresh replication, once |
| Local ledger, new days | 2026-11-13 onward | about 5,500 per day across boards | Untouched |

**One-minute candles.** `~/.moonwalking/data/candle_archive.sqlite` holds
Coinbase Exchange one-minute candles for 407 products from 2026-09-06 to
2026-10-12 and hourly candles from 2025-10-01 to 2026-10-12. Nothing later has
been pulled. `backend/candle_archive.py` is the existing puller; the source is
public and needs no credentials. A minute with no trades has no row.

## Candidate signals for "still building" against "already spent"

1. Speeding up or slowing down: the share of the 3-minute move made in the last
   minute.
2. Size against the coin's own normal movement.
3. Trading volume behind the move, against one or two prints.
4. Persistence: first appearance, or repeated returns to the board.
5. Coin-specific against market-wide movement.

Signals 1, 3 and 4 can be built from stored fields. Signals 2 and 5 need
one-minute candles for the same period.

## Board options under discussion

1. Activity gate: rank by percentage change, but require enough trading.
2. Staircase against spike: rank or mark by how the move was built.
3. Blended ranking: percentage change adjusted by proven signals.
4. Rank by how unusual the move is for that coin.
5. Repeat-runner view over the last hour.
6. Early-watch list for coins not yet on a board. This needs whole-universe
   data; board episodes cannot test it.

The owner's direction is to combine the best pieces of these options into one
working design, not to choose a single option.

## Fixed constraints

- The boards stay a live list of what is moving most. Any change must still read
  that way to a user.
- No live score, ranking, weight or production change follows from a study
  without a separate review.
- The repository is public. Account data, balances and holdings stay out of it.
- The locked historical validation is not rerun to answer its original question.
===== END SOURCE 01 =====

===== BEGIN SOURCE 02/16: docs/research/LIVE_MOVERS_FRESH_REPLICATION_PROTOCOL_20261113.md =====
# Live movers: fresh-data replication of the frozen ranker — protocol, 2026-11-13

**Status: predeclared. Committed before any outcome in the fresh window was read.**
Only row counts, schema and label presence were inspected while writing this.
The run is allowed once. Its result is reported whatever it shows.

## Why this test

The [locked historical comparison](LIVE_MOVERS_RESULTS_20261027.md) asked whether
the nonlinear model M1 beat the simple model B2. That was inconclusive. The same
results contain a larger pattern that was never claimed because it was a
secondary comparison on a window that was not an untouched holdout:

- The stored live score (B1) and the displayed percentage move (B0) had negative
  within-day rank correlation with the forward directional return in all six
  board and horizon cells.
- The simple frozen price and volume ridge (B2) was positive in all six.
- On the primary cell, B2 minus B1 was +0.061 with a three-day block interval of
  +0.038 to +0.082.

F1–F5 cannot be tested yet: no multi-day 5s shadow sample exists. This pattern
can be tested now, because the model was frozen on 2026-10-27 and the local
collector has kept recording board episodes since then.

## Hypothesis

On board-entry episodes recorded after the model freeze, the frozen B2 ranker
orders five-minute outcomes better than the stored live score, by at least the
project's existing minimum effect of 0.02 IC, and B2's own rank correlation is
positive.

## Data

- Source: local ledger `~/.moonwalking/data/signal_outcomes.sqlite`, table
  `board_outcomes_v2`, read with `mode=ro` under one read transaction and copied
  unrevised to a private capture outside Git. The capture hash is recorded.
- **Primary holdout:** `entered_ts` from 2026-10-28T00:00Z up to, not including,
  2026-11-13T00:00Z. Every row is after the 2026-10-27 freeze and validation run.
- **Secondary window:** 2026-10-18T00:00Z to 2026-10-28T00:00Z, with the
  contract's 1,800s purge before its end. These rows are after the historical
  validation window and were never modelled. They are reported separately and do
  not affect the verdict.
- Eligibility: the frozen `freeze_live_contract.classify`, unchanged, run over
  the whole local ledger in `entered_ts,id` order so the 3,600s cooldown state
  is exact. Same snapshot and model version, same required inputs, both labels
  finite. Rejection counts by reason are reported.
- This is a different host from the production backup used historically. That
  is a limitation and also a harder replication.

## Models

B0, B1, B2 and M1 exactly as in
[the frozen specification](results/live_movers_contract_20261027/COMPARISON_SPEC.json).
The result file stores coefficients but not the winsor limits and scaling, so
B2 and M1 are refitted on the frozen train plus discovery rows with the frozen
alphas. The run refuses unless every refitted coefficient equals the stored one
to six decimals in all six cells. The locked validation rows are not scored
again. No parameter is fitted, selected or adjusted on the fresh data.

## Primary statistic and decision rule

Cell: `confirmation_3m_up/up`, `gross_return_5m`.

Statistic: mean across UTC days of the paired within-day Spearman
IC(B2) − IC(B1). A day counts with at least 20 rows and a nonconstant target and
predictions for all four models, so every model uses identical days and rows.
Days are weighted equally. Intervals are 95%: paired daily bootstrap and
three-calendar-day moving blocks, 5,000 replicates each, seed 20261113.

| Call | Condition |
|---|---|
| **Keep** | Difference ≥ +0.02, and both interval lower bounds > 0, and mean daily IC(B2) > 0 |
| **Drop** | Difference ≤ 0, or both interval upper bounds < +0.02 |
| **Inconclusive** | Anything else |
| **Insufficient** | Fewer than 12 scored days in the primary holdout; no call is made |

"Keep" means the ordering claim replicated on fresh data. It supports proposing
a board-ranking change for review. It is not a trading claim and changes no
live score, weight or production behavior.

## Secondary readouts, reported and not gated

1. Mean daily IC of B0, B1, B2 and M1 with both intervals, and the count of days
   on which each is positive.
2. The same paired differences in the other five cells and in the secondary
   window.
3. M1 − B2 on the fresh primary cell: the original preregistered question, on
   new data. Reported with its intervals; it does not change this test's call.
4. Rank-band tables for each ranker on the primary holdout: mean gross
   five-minute outcome per band, with the contract's 0.2, 0.5, 0.9 and 1.2%
   round-trip scenarios, and top band minus bottom band.

The prior expectation from the historical top bands is that no band clears
costs. A positive ranking result with negative band means is a ranking finding
and will be described that way.

## Limits fixed in advance

- Board-entry episodes with last-trade labels. No executable fills, spreads or
  whole-universe replay.
- One market stretch of about sixteen days. Regime coverage is narrow.
- Same-coin episodes on different boards are not independent. Strata are never
  pooled.
- No rerun, no second window, no changed threshold after the result is seen.
  A failed or interrupted attempt is recorded and blocks another run.
- The locked historical validation, the F1–F5 work, Codex's checkpoint files and
  every other worktree stay untouched.
===== END SOURCE 02 =====

===== BEGIN SOURCE 03/16: docs/research/LIVE_MOVERS_FRESH_REPLICATION_RESULTS_20261113.md =====
# Live movers: fresh-data replication of the frozen ranker — results, 2026-11-13

**Call: keep.** On sixteen days recorded after the model freeze, the frozen
simple price and volume ranker (B2) ordered five-minute outcomes on the 3m-up
board better than the stored live score (B1) by +0.049 IC. The
[predeclared rule](LIVE_MOVERS_FRESH_REPLICATION_PROTOCOL_20261113.md) needed
+0.02 with both interval lower bounds above zero and a positive B2 correlation.
All four conditions held. The block interval's lower bound is +0.004, so the
pass is real but not wide.

This is a ranking finding. No band of any ranker had a positive mean gross
outcome, and nothing clears the smallest cost scenario. It changes no live
score, weight or production behavior.

## Primary result

Cell `confirmation_3m_up/up`, `gross_return_5m`, 2026-10-28 to 2026-11-12 UTC,
26,648 eligible episodes, 16 scored days. One run, from clean commit `7102185`.

| Measure | Result | Daily bootstrap 95% | Three-day block 95% | Days positive |
|---|---:|---|---|---:|
| **B2 − B1, primary** | **+0.0493** | +0.0134 to +0.0845 | +0.0043 to +0.0832 | 13 of 16 |
| Mean daily IC, B2 | +0.0260 | +0.0088 to +0.0424 | +0.0059 to +0.0395 | 14 of 16 |
| Mean daily IC, live score B1 | −0.0233 | −0.0429 to −0.0040 | −0.0446 to +0.0022 | 6 of 16 |
| Mean daily IC, percentage move B0 | −0.0261 | −0.0407 to −0.0109 | −0.0366 to −0.0071 | 2 of 16 |

The historical values were B2 +0.033, B1 −0.028 and a difference of +0.061.
The fresh values are a little smaller and have the same signs.

## What the ordering means in outcomes

Mean recorded gross five-minute outcome by rank band, lowest rank to highest:

| Ranker | Band 1 | Band 2 | Band 3 | Band 4 | Band 5 | Top minus bottom |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| B0 percentage move | −0.037% | −0.025% | −0.033% | −0.039% | −0.183% | −0.147% |
| B1 live score | −0.047% | −0.025% | −0.031% | −0.057% | −0.167% | −0.120% |
| B2 frozen ridge | −0.178% | −0.050% | −0.024% | −0.028% | −0.037% | +0.141% |
| M1 frozen nonlinear | −0.181% | −0.051% | −0.035% | −0.027% | −0.024% | +0.158% |

The effect sits in one band. Board entries with the highest live score or the
largest displayed move gave back about 0.17–0.18% over the next five minutes.
The other four fifths averaged −0.02% to −0.06%. At fifteen minutes the top
live-score band averaged −0.27% against −0.05% to −0.08% for the rest.

B2 mostly identifies that same group and ranks it last. Its own top band is no
better than the middle. The supported statement is an overextension warning:
the highest-scored entries on the mover boards retrace the most. It is not
evidence that any group goes on to rise.

## Every declared cell, both windows

B2 − B1 was positive in all twelve cells. B0 and B1 were negative in all twelve.

| Window | Stratum | Horizon | Episodes | Days | B1 IC | B2 IC | B2 − B1 | Block 95% | Days positive |
|---|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---|---:|
| Primary | ignition_1m/up | 5m | 30,356 | 16 | −0.033 | +0.035 | +0.068 | +0.032 to +0.088 | 14/16 |
| Primary | ignition_1m/up | 15m | 30,356 | 16 | −0.072 | +0.067 | +0.140 | +0.100 to +0.178 | 16/16 |
| Primary | confirmation_3m_up/up | **5m** | 26,648 | 16 | −0.023 | +0.026 | **+0.049** | +0.004 to +0.083 | 13/16 |
| Primary | confirmation_3m_up/up | 15m | 26,648 | 16 | −0.062 | +0.077 | +0.139 | +0.092 to +0.188 | 15/16 |
| Primary | confirmation_3m_down/down | 5m | 26,563 | 16 | −0.031 | +0.029 | +0.059 | +0.017 to +0.120 | 13/16 |
| Primary | confirmation_3m_down/down | 15m | 26,563 | 16 | −0.077 | +0.085 | +0.162 | +0.120 to +0.221 | 16/16 |
| Oct 18–27 | ignition_1m/up | 5m | 20,047 | 10 | −0.027 | +0.024 | +0.051 | +0.022 to +0.070 | 8/10 |
| Oct 18–27 | ignition_1m/up | 15m | 20,047 | 10 | −0.068 | +0.051 | +0.120 | +0.096 to +0.130 | 10/10 |
| Oct 18–27 | confirmation_3m_up/up | 5m | 17,105 | 10 | −0.019 | +0.022 | +0.040 | +0.026 to +0.050 | 8/10 |
| Oct 18–27 | confirmation_3m_up/up | 15m | 17,105 | 10 | −0.051 | +0.065 | +0.116 | +0.099 to +0.125 | 10/10 |
| Oct 18–27 | confirmation_3m_down/down | 5m | 17,186 | 10 | −0.022 | +0.014 | +0.036 | +0.002 to +0.058 | 8/10 |
| Oct 18–27 | confirmation_3m_down/down | 15m | 17,186 | 10 | −0.056 | +0.064 | +0.120 | +0.102 to +0.127 | 10/10 |

The fifteen-minute cells are the strongest: the gap is +0.12 to +0.16 and B1
was negative on at least fifteen of sixteen primary days in each. Fifteen
minutes was not the predeclared primary and does not change the call. Strata
are not pooled; the same coin can appear on more than one board.

## The original question, on new data

M1 − B2 on the fresh primary cell was +0.0152, daily interval +0.0031 to
+0.0275, block interval +0.0059 to +0.0248, positive on 14 of 16 days. The
historical value was +0.0127. It is again positive and again below the +0.02
minimum effect. In the other five primary-window cells it ranged from −0.004 to
+0.008. The nonlinear extension remains unproven; the simple model carries the
result.

## What does not hold up

- **No tradable edge is shown.** Every band of every ranker has a negative mean
  gross outcome. The best band is −0.024% before any cost.
- **Not every day.** On 2026-11-07 the signs reversed (B1 +0.062, B2 −0.063),
  and from 2026-11-08 to 2026-11-11 the daily difference was between −0.023 and
  +0.009. Nine of the sixteen daily differences exceed +0.02.
- **Last-trade labels.** A bid/ask bounce after a fast move could produce some
  of the giveback. The effect grows from five to fifteen minutes, which a
  bounce alone would not do, but no quotes were recorded to separate them.
- **One host, about sixteen days.** The local ledger differs from the
  production backup used historically. Regime coverage is narrow.
- **Board entries only.** Nothing here describes coins that never reach a
  board, warning lead time, or F1–F5.

## Provenance

- Protocol committed at `671b80f` before any outcome in the window was read;
  driver at `175078d` and `37e6c78`; outcome-blind verification at `7102185`.
- [Verification](results/live_movers_fresh_replication_20261113/results_verify_fresh_replication.json):
  frozen inputs match their manifest, and refitted B2 and M1 coefficients equal
  the stored ones to six decimals in all six cells. The first capture copied
  183,347 ledger rows, SHA-256 `7a3eca14…ea82c`; it stays outside Git. The
  recorded `code_dirty: true` at verification was the verification file itself
  being rewritten; the code hashes are pinned and matched at the test.

===== BEGIN SOURCE 05/16: docs/research/LIVE_MOVERS_RESULTS_20261027.md =====
# Live movers: frozen historical comparison - 2026-10-27

**Decision: inconclusive under the preregistered primary gate.** The nonlinear
model M1 improves five-minute ranking over the simpler B2 by 0.01265 IC on
the 3m-up board. This is below the 0.02 minimum effect, and the three-day
block interval includes zero. This result does not support a live score or
ranking change. No retuning or second validation run was performed.

## Primary result

Primary population: `confirmation_3m_up/up`; target: gross five-minute
directional return. There are **24,457 eligible episodes across 14 UTC days**
(2026-10-04 through 2026-10-17). Every model uses the same rows and days;
there were no additional daily-group exclusions. IC is within-day Spearman
rank correlation, averaged equally across days; it is not a return percentage.

| Primary measure | Result |
|---|---:|
| Mean daily IC, percentage-move baseline B0 | -0.03392 |
| Mean daily IC, current live-score baseline B1 | -0.02819 |
| Mean daily IC, regularized price/volume model B2 | +0.03280 |
| Mean daily IC, nonlinear extension M1 | +0.04545 |
| Primary difference M1 minus B2 | +0.01265 |
| Paired daily-bootstrap 95% interval | [+0.00159, +0.02393] |
| Three-day moving-block 95% interval | [-0.00047, +0.02545] |
| Discovery difference M1 minus B2 | +0.01311 |
| Required minimum primary difference | +0.02000 |
| Preregistered verdict | **inconclusive** |

Both intervals use 5000 replicates and seed 20261027. Discovery was positive,
but the validation effect-size and block-interval conditions did not pass.
The more favorable comparisons against the existing score do not replace
the preregistered M1-versus-B2 primary test.

## Absolute outcomes and cost scenarios

Higher rank correlation is not evidence of positive executable returns.
The primary population's highest score band had the following **mean recorded
gross five-minute outcomes**. Bands use average-rank percentiles and keep
equal scores together; B1's top-band count therefore differs.

| Ranker, top band | Episodes | Gross mean | Minus 0.2% | Minus 0.5% | Minus 0.9% | Minus 1.2% |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| B0 percentage move | 4,891 | -0.15630% | -0.35630% | -0.65630% | -1.05630% | -1.35630% |
| B1 current score | 4,800 | -0.15011% | -0.35011% | -0.65011% | -1.05011% | -1.35011% |
| B2 price/volume | 4,891 | -0.04345% | -0.24345% | -0.54345% | -0.94345% | -1.24345% |
| M1 nonlinear | 4,891 | -0.03006% | -0.23006% | -0.53006% | -0.93006% | -1.23006% |

These are episode-level scenario means, not portfolio P&L or executable fills.
Costs are subtracted once from gross labels; stored already-net outcomes are
not used. No historical spread or fill path was reconstructed. In this
primary test, the fitted models rank relatively less-adverse observations
above more-adverse ones, while even their highest band has a negative mean.

## All declared strata and horizons

These are separate descriptive/secondary results; do not pool them or choose
a new primary result after seeing them. All six comparisons retain all
14 validation days and all eligible rows in the stated stratum.

| Stratum | Forward horizon | Episodes | B0 IC | B1 IC | B2 IC | M1 IC | M1-B2 |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| ignition_1m/up | 5m | 28,061 | -0.03756 | -0.03810 | +0.03169 | +0.02358 | -0.00811 |
| ignition_1m/up | 15m | 28,061 | -0.07411 | -0.07791 | +0.06234 | +0.06439 | +0.00204 |
| confirmation_3m_up/up | **5m primary** | 24,457 | -0.03392 | -0.02819 | +0.03280 | +0.04545 | +0.01265 |
| confirmation_3m_up/up | 15m | 24,457 | -0.07407 | -0.06565 | +0.07320 | +0.06882 | -0.00437 |
| confirmation_3m_down/down | 5m | 24,586 | -0.02128 | -0.02493 | +0.02371 | +0.02602 | +0.00231 |
| confirmation_3m_down/down | 15m | 24,586 | -0.05573 | -0.06595 | +0.05974 | +0.06258 | +0.00284 |

There is no short-selling conclusion from downside directional returns.
Neither fitted model's primary display scores saturated at 0 or 100 in this
sample. Complete bands, coefficients, per-day ICs, selected alphas and paired
comparisons are retained in the machine-readable artifacts below.

## Provenance, limits and next step

- [Frozen comparison specification](results/live_movers_contract_20261027/COMPARISON_SPEC.json)
  and [data contract](LIVE_MOVERS_DATA_CONTRACT_20261027.md) were unchanged.
- The [pre-fit review](LIVE_MOVERS_DRIVER_REVIEW_20261027.md) and corrected
  driver are committed at `7a55922`; clean-code verification is in
  [`results_verify_live_movers.json`](results/live_movers_comparison/results_verify_live_movers.json).
- Discovery ran on clean commit `5db135a`. Its choices were committed and
  pushed at `9747f94` **before** validation. The matching input/code hashes
  and selected settings are in
  [`results_discovery_live_movers.json`](results/live_movers_comparison/results_discovery_live_movers.json).
- The one-time validation ran from clean commit `9747f94`; see
  [`results_test_live_movers.json`](results/live_movers_comparison/results_test_live_movers.json).
  An exclusive attempt record remains in the local analysis directory and
  either the local or committed result blocks another run. NumPy 2.5.3 and
  pandas 3.0.6 were recorded for both phases.

Result QA confirms identical code/input hashes across verify/discovery/test,
matching frozen alphas, all six common-cohort daily means, row/band totals,
and single cost subtraction. All 11 frozen artifact hashes still match;
an existing real result refuses another attempt. The 23 targeted tests,
repository guardrails, report links and diff checks pass.

The cohort is persisted board-entry episodes with last-trade labels, not a
whole-universe replay. Earlier research may have examined overlapping dates;
this is locked historical validation, **not an untouched holdout**. Fourteen
validation days provide limited market-regime coverage. The ordinal score
is not a probability, and this study does not measure 30s warning lead time.

Keep the current scoring behavior while preserving these results. The next
evidence source is the separately running Coinbase side/coverage diagnostic,
followed, if its gates pass, by prospective 5s recording and the fixed-window
shadow study. Do not rerun this validation to search for a more favorable
formula or extend it with retrospective microstructure.
===== END SOURCE 05 =====

===== BEGIN SOURCE 06/16: docs/research/LIVE_MOVERS_DATA_CONTRACT_20261027.md =====
# Live movers: frozen contract and retention fix — 2026-10-27

**The contract is published and the production-backup cohort passes readiness.**
The missing board metadata is fixed and regression-tested on a separate branch
based on `runtime/collector` at `ed3e231`. The running collector and dashboard
have not been changed; the fix takes effect for new rows after deployment.
No forecast has been fitted and no outcome performance has been calculated here.

**Prospective proposal review (2026-10-27):** the pasted 30s/3m early-warning
proposal is a separate, future cohort. Its corrected collection, smoothing,
eligibility and evaluation rules are in
[`LIVE_MOVERS_PROSPECTIVE_CONTRACT_20261027.md`](LIVE_MOVERS_PROSPECTIVE_CONTRACT_20261027.md).
That addendum requires an hour-long `ticker`/`matches`/`heartbeat` audit before
micro-bar implementation. It does not amend the frozen historical JSON
contracts, eligible episodes, hashes, model comparison or date splits below.

Claude can build the separate short-horizon driver against the exact artifacts
below. Keep the daily/fundamentals experiment on its own branch. Its remote tag
`research/fundamentals-driver-2026-10-26` was verified to dereference to `058d740`;
this checkpoint does not approve that separate comparison driver.

## Authoritative inputs

- [`CONTRACT_PRODUCTION.json`](results/live_movers_contract_20261027/CONTRACT_PRODUCTION.json): episodes, timing, features, minimum history and date splits.
- [`COMPARISON_SPEC.json`](results/live_movers_contract_20261027/COMPARISON_SPEC.json): fixed comparison, model forms, primary statistic and reporting.
- [`PRODUCTION_CAPTURE_MANIFEST.json`](results/live_movers_contract_20261027/PRODUCTION_CAPTURE_MANIFEST.json): exact frozen research DB/eligible-list hashes.
- [`PRODUCTION_COVERAGE.json`](results/live_movers_contract_20261027/PRODUCTION_COVERAGE.json): readiness, rejection counts and non-null features by UTC day.
- [`STRATUM_COVERAGE.json`](results/live_movers_contract_20261027/STRATUM_COVERAGE.json): phase/board/direction counts.

The immutable capture on this PC is
`C:\Users\tgpet\.moonwalking\analysis\live_movers_contract_20261027_production\`:
`board_episodes.sqlite` (`episodes` table), `eligible_episodes.csv`, its copied
data contract, and `MANIFEST.json`. Every file is SHA-256 recorded and read-only.
Raw public-market records stay outside Git. Git carries their manifest and the
complete contract. Use the CSV's `id` to join to the **same frozen DB**, not the
running ledger or another backup's integer IDs.

## Why production is the primary cohort

The first consistent capture of the local ledger retained 90,518 episodes before
2026-10-27 00:00 UTC. Despite its calendar span, eligible training covered only
six days and discovery only one. It **fails** the unchanged 21/7/14-day minimums.
Its capture and [`COVERAGE.json`](results/live_movers_contract_20261027/COVERAGE.json)
are preserved. It is not merged with production or used to fill production gaps.

Before inspecting any outcome magnitudes, amendment 1 selected the existing
production backup `prod-backup/20261018-0109/signal_outcomes.sqlite`, using its
last complete UTC day as the decision cutoff. The minima, feature rules and
episode policy were retained. `freeze_live_contract.py` copied 495,539 rows
under an explicit read-only SQLite read transaction, preserving their values.
The readiness report uses feature/label **availability**, not return values.

| Phase | Decision dates, UTC | Eligible episodes | Observed days | Products |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|
| Train | 2026-07-19 to 2026-09-26, with end purge | 217,302 | 40 | 192 |
| Discovery | 2026-09-27 to 2026-10-03, with end purge | 38,764 | 7 | 119 |
| Historical validation | 2026-10-04 to 2026-10-17 | 77,104 | 14 | 205 |

All three strata (`ignition_1m/up`, `confirmation_3m_up/up`,
`confirmation_3m_down/down`) cover every reported observed day in their phase.
Snapshot fields start on **2026-08-18**; earlier rows are not invented or filled.

**Historical limitation:** earlier research examined overlapping dates and may
have inspected these outcomes. This is a locked historical comparison, **not an
untouched holdout**. Fourteen validation days also give limited regime coverage.
Fresh prospective confirmation after a model freeze is required for any live
release. Row count alone is not independent evidence.

## Episode, identity and timing rules

1. The unit is a persisted board-entry episode, identified by frozen DB `id`,
   `board`, canonical `product_id`, `direction` and `entered_ts`. It describes
   the first **server board observation**, not the user's browser impression.
2. Existing board tracking records top-eight membership and normally permits
   re-entry one hour after exit. Historical environment settings were not
   retained, so do not assume those settings prove every old episode's spacing.
   Apply an additional deterministic rolling **3,600-second entry cooldown per
   `(board, product_id)`**, keeping the earliest record before checking feature
   completeness. Never select a later, more complete duplicate instead.
3. Keep board/direction strata separate. Same-coin appearances on different
   boards are not independent replications. The primary test is
   `confirmation_3m_up/up`; other strata are secondary and reported separately.
4. Preserve exact historical USD product IDs, including delisted products. No
   mapping from today's listing universe, symbol renaming, RNDR/RENDER or
   JUP/JUPITER splicing is allowed. The study does not join external token data.
5. `captured_ts` must be present, no later than entry and at most **60 seconds**
   before entry. It is the ranking computation start, not proof of the last
   exchange trade's freshness or of an atomic cross-store observation.
6. Eligible snapshots have `live-strength-v1-snapshot-v2` and model
   `live-strength-v1`; version 3 forward captures are a different cohort.
7. Split intervals are start-inclusive/end-exclusive. Remove **30 minutes**
   before the next phase boundary so 15-minute labels and observation slack
   cannot straddle training/discovery or discovery/test.

## Features and labels

| Field | Availability in this production capture | Phase-1 treatment |
|---|---|---|
| `pct_1m`, `pct_3m`, `volume_change_1h_pct` | Recorded from Aug 18 through Oct 17; daily counts in the report | Required finite values; reject missing rows |
| Stored `live_score`, board `start_change_pct` | Retained entry-time baselines | Compare current score and displayed move ranking on identical eligible episodes |
| `pct_1h`, `trend_streak` | Partially populated over the same period | Profiled, excluded from the primary formula; complete-case secondary comparisons rerun all baselines on that same subset |
| Quote-volume USD and realized-volatility numeric metadata | Zero non-null board values in the production capture | Forward-only after the retention fix; never retroactively fill old snapshots |
| Liquidity/pressure/market-relation categories | Defaults and measurements not fully distinguishable in old snapshots | Excluded from primary features; neither `normal` nor `neutral` proves measurement |
| Spread, numeric taker delta, market-relative return | Point-in-time numeric coverage not established | Excluded from this first experiment |

Snapshot `raw_inputs` are authoritative; do not reconstruct missing features
from later APIs. Historical 1m/3m fields are nominal past-return features: their
exact baseline ages were not stored, so a derived acceleration is a nominal
proxy, not a verified exact wall-clock interval. The fixed primary model uses
only the three required inputs and its declared nonlinear transforms.

Both `gross_return_5m` and `gross_return_15m` must be finite for the common
cohort. This is a label-observability rule, not conditioning on their sign or
size. The primary target is five-minute gross directional return; 15 minutes
is secondary. Existing last-trade grades do **not** certify executable fills
or per-checkpoint latency. Record these limits in every result.

Use the frozen gross fields for the cost scenarios (0.2/0.5/0.9/1.2% round trip).
Stored `return_*` fields have estimated costs already deducted: do not subtract
them again. Historical bid/ask bounce is a plausible confound, not a measured
explanation of the earlier score's negative relationship. No profitability or
short-selling claim follows from the downside board's directional labels.

## The 30-second ruling

**Exclude 30-second inputs from this historical comparison.** The local price
tape retains roughly one day at irregular intervals; the archived candles are
60s/3600s. They cannot establish a long, exact sub-minute event history. A refresh
timer is not a return window and an OHLC bar does not reveal its internal path.

A prospective 30-second feature is possible only after retaining sufficient
event/receipt timestamps, price source and baseline-age/coverage information.
Its allowed endpoint ages and storage budget must be frozen before collection.
No interpolation or retrospective synthetic 30-second series is permitted.
This task does not start another collector or change retention on port 5003.

## Retention-path fix and regression evidence

The actual function is `build_live_rankings()`. The board call already passed
price, volume, context and alerts; it omitted only the optional matching maps.
It now reads `realized_volatility_by_product()` and
`latest_quote_volume_by_product()` at **the board calculation's own timestamp**,
then supplies both maps. It does not reuse the earlier signal sample.
Each provider failure degrades independently to missing metadata.

`live_ranking.py` adds the hourly quote-volume bucket timestamp and
`context_input_supplied` flags in **snapshot v3**. Supplied means a non-null
context field was passed; it does not by itself certify an actual measurement.
Legacy scoring defaults remain compatible while their provenance is explicit
for future research. New fields stay in `raw_inputs` and are stripped from the
public payload. Model version and score weights remain unchanged.

Tests execute the **actual board call-site statements**, check provider times,
store and reread the board snapshot, and verify original snapshots remain
immutable on later observations. An integration case uses real temporary
price/volume stores and their real provider functions. Missing/failing provider
cases retain nulls. Published rankings, scores, data quality and payloads match
the pre-enrichment calculation.

The running collector still uses its current checkout; this tested branch has
not been deployed or used to repair historical rows. Apply the patch through a
reviewed runtime update, preserving that worktree and following its service
handoff. Do not run a second backend on its port.

## Source-audit reconciliation

The new Gemini attachment is retained as `SOURCE_AUDIT_SUBMISSION.md`; its claims
are evidence candidates, not instructions. Its closed-bin and historical
microstructure cautions are useful. Several statements need qualification:

- The code has in-memory quote/trade context in `coinbase_ws.py`; it does not
  simply discard every quote or side. Persistent historical coverage is the
  separate missing piece.
- Coinbase documents maker-side semantics for Exchange `match` and REST trades;
  do not automatically generalize a channel's interpretation to a differently
  named API. [Exchange channels](https://docs.cdp.coinbase.com/exchange/websocket-feed/channels),
  [REST trades](https://docs.cdp.coinbase.com/api-reference/exchange-api/rest-api/products/get-product-trades).
- Exchange `ticker_batch` has the same schema as Exchange `ticker`; Advanced
  Trade is a different feed. Coinbase describes L2 REST as the **full aggregated
  book**, not a guaranteed top-50-level response.
  [Exchange channels](https://docs.cdp.coinbase.com/exchange/websocket-feed/channels),
  [product book](https://docs.cdp.coinbase.com/api-reference/exchange-api/rest-api/products/get-product-book).
- A closed historical candle is causally settled, but is not proof that the
  application fetched it before that episode. Candle enrichment is outside
  this first frozen comparison. One candle's close and the next candle's open
  also need not be equal.

## Claude's concrete next step

Build the separate driver now, using the **production** contract and capture,
not the failed local cohort or a mutable live ledger. The fixed comparison is:
percentage-move baseline, current live score, a regularized price/volume model,
and its declared nonlinear extension. Select regularization on discovery,
freeze inputs/code and run the historical validation once. The JSON comparison
spec defines the exact primary statistic, 0.02 minimum effect, uncertainty and
ordinal 0–100 score mapping. A score is not a probability.

Refuse fitting if the capture hashes, eligible-row identity, readiness,
contracts or required feature schema differ. No comparison code needs to wait
for the forward retention fix to be deployed: the primary historical features
are already present. The richer forward cohort must be evaluated separately.

Outcome performance, fundamentals-driver approval, live deployment and any
change to dashboard ranking remain separate work. This checkpoint delivers
the missing trace, tested fix, frozen episode/data rules and 30-second ruling.

===== BEGIN SOURCE 09/16: backend/board_outcomes.py =====
"""Persistent outcome measurement for the public mover boards.

An observation begins when a product enters one of the published board cohorts.
The same entry is then measured at 5, 15, 30, and 60 minutes using directional
returns (positive means the displayed move continued, negative means it
reversed).  This module intentionally reports samples and uncertainty instead
of turning a short-horizon mover into trading advice.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import math
import os
from pathlib import Path
from statistics import median
import sqlite3
import threading
import time
from typing import Any

from live_ranking import build_feature_snapshot
from outcome_gaps import HEARTBEAT_TABLE_SQL, INTERRUPTED, GapDetector, open_across_gap


DEFAULT_DB_PATH = Path(__file__).resolve().parent / "data" / "signal_outcomes.sqlite"
CHECKPOINTS = (
    (300, "return_5m"),
    (900, "return_15m"),
    (1800, "return_30m"),
    (3600, "return_60m"),
)
BOARD_NAMES = ("ignition_1m", "confirmation_3m_up", "confirmation_3m_down")
MODEL_VERSION = "board-outcomes-v2"
TABLE_NAME = "board_outcomes_v2"
_STABLECOINS = {"USDC", "USDT", "DAI", "PYUSD", "GUSD", "USDS", "EURC"}


def _number(value: Any) -> float | None:
    try:
        if value is None or isinstance(value, bool):
            return None
        parsed = float(value)
        return parsed if math.isfinite(parsed) else None
    except (TypeError, ValueError):
        return None


def _symbol(value: Any) -> str:
    raw = str(value or "").strip().upper()
    for suffix in ("-USD", "-USDT", "-USDC", "-PERP"):
        if raw.endswith(suffix):
            return raw[: -len(suffix)]
    return raw


def _wilson_interval(
    successes: int, total: int, z: float = 1.96
) -> tuple[float | None, float | None]:
    if total <= 0:
        return None, None
    proportion = successes / total
    denominator = 1.0 + (z * z / total)
    centre = proportion + (z * z / (2.0 * total))
    margin = z * math.sqrt(
        (proportion * (1.0 - proportion) / total) + (z * z / (4.0 * total * total))
    )
    return (centre - margin) / denominator, (centre + margin) / denominator


class BoardOutcomeStore:
    def __init__(self, db_path: str | Path | None = None):
        configured = os.getenv("MW_BOARD_OUTCOMES_DB") or os.getenv(
            "MW_SIGNAL_OUTCOMES_DB"
        )
        self.db_path = Path(db_path or configured or DEFAULT_DB_PATH)
        self.move_threshold_pct = max(
            0.05, float(os.getenv("MW_BOARD_MOVE_THRESHOLD_PCT", "0.5"))
        )
        self.horizon_seconds = max(
            3600, int(os.getenv("MW_BOARD_OUTCOME_HORIZON_SECONDS", "3600"))
        )
        self.visible_limit = max(1, int(os.getenv("MW_BOARD_TRACKED_ROWS", "8")))
        self.cooldown_seconds = max(
            60, int(os.getenv("MW_BOARD_REENTRY_COOLDOWN_SECONDS", "3600"))
        )
        self.round_trip_cost_pct = max(
            0.0, float(os.getenv("MW_BOARD_ROUND_TRIP_COST_PCT", "0.20"))
        )
        self.minimum_samples = max(
            30, int(os.getenv("MW_BOARD_MIN_INDEPENDENT_SAMPLES", "100"))
        )
        self.minimum_span_days = max(1, int(os.getenv("MW_BOARD_MIN_SPAN_DAYS", "14")))
        self._gaps = GapDetector("board_outcomes")
        self._init_lock = threading.Lock()
        self._summary_lock = threading.Lock()
        self._summary_cache: tuple[float, dict[str, Any]] | None = None
        self.ensure_db()

    def _connect(self) -> sqlite3.Connection:
        self.db_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
        conn = sqlite3.connect(self.db_path, timeout=8, check_same_thread=False)
        conn.row_factory = sqlite3.Row
        conn.execute("PRAGMA journal_mode=WAL")
        conn.execute("PRAGMA busy_timeout=8000")
        return conn

    def ensure_db(self) -> None:
        with self._init_lock:
            conn = self._connect()
            try:
                conn.executescript(
                    f"""
                    CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS {TABLE_NAME} (
                        id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT,
                        model_version TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT '{MODEL_VERSION}',
                        board TEXT NOT NULL,
                        product_id TEXT NOT NULL,
                        symbol TEXT NOT NULL,
                        direction TEXT NOT NULL,
                        entered_ts INTEGER NOT NULL,
                        last_seen_ts INTEGER NOT NULL,
                        exited_ts INTEGER,
                        start_price REAL NOT NULL,
                        entry_rank INTEGER,
                        start_change_pct REAL,
                        control_product_id TEXT,
                        control_start_price REAL,
                        control_change_pct REAL,
                        estimated_cost_pct REAL NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
                        gross_return_5m REAL,
                        gross_return_15m REAL,
                        gross_return_30m REAL,
                        gross_return_60m REAL,
                        return_5m REAL,
                        return_15m REAL,
                        return_30m REAL,
                        return_60m REAL,
                        control_return_5m REAL,
                        control_return_15m REAL,
                        control_return_30m REAL,
                        control_return_60m REAL,
                        max_favorable_15m REAL NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
                        max_adverse_15m REAL NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
                        max_favorable_pct REAL NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
                        max_adverse_pct REAL NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
                        outcome_15m TEXT,
                        complete INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
                        UNIQUE(board, product_id, entered_ts)
                    );
                    CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_board_outcomes_v2_history
                        ON {TABLE_NAME}(board, direction, outcome_15m, entered_ts);
                    CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_board_outcomes_v2_collecting
                        ON {TABLE_NAME}(complete, entered_ts);
                    CREATE UNIQUE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_board_outcomes_v2_active
                        ON {TABLE_NAME}(board, product_id)
                        WHERE exited_ts IS NULL;
                    """
                )
                # Safe additive migration: no-op on a fresh DB; adds columns to
                # existing DBs without disturbing current rows (they remain NULL).
                existing = {
                    row[1] for row in conn.execute(f"PRAGMA table_info({TABLE_NAME})")
                }
                for col_def in (
                    "feature_schema_version TEXT",
                    "feature_snapshot_json TEXT",
                ):
                    if col_def.split()[0] not in existing:
                        conn.execute(f"ALTER TABLE {TABLE_NAME} ADD COLUMN {col_def}")
                conn.execute(HEARTBEAT_TABLE_SQL)
                conn.commit()
            finally:
                conn.close()

    @staticmethod
    def _directional_return(direction: str, start: float, current: float) -> float:
        raw = ((current / start) - 1.0) * 100.0
        return -raw if direction == "down" else raw

    @staticmethod
    def _row_product(row: dict[str, Any]) -> tuple[str, str]:
        product_id = (
            str(row.get("product_id") or row.get("productId") or "").strip().upper()
        )
        symbol = _symbol(row.get("symbol") or row.get("ticker") or product_id)
        if not product_id and symbol:
            product_id = f"{symbol}-USD"
        return product_id, symbol

    @staticmethod
    def _price_from_row(
        row: dict[str, Any], prices: dict[str, Any], product_id: str, symbol: str
    ) -> float | None:
        direct = _number(
            row.get("current_price")
            if row.get("current_price") is not None
            else row.get("price")
        )
        if direct is not None and direct > 0:
            return direct
        for key in (product_id, symbol):
            candidate = prices.get(key)
            if isinstance(candidate, dict):
                candidate = candidate.get("price") or candidate.get("current_price")
            parsed = _number(candidate)
            if parsed is not None and parsed > 0:
                return parsed
        return None

    @staticmethod
    def _current_price(
        prices: dict[str, Any], product_id: str, symbol: str
    ) -> float | None:
        for key in (product_id, symbol):
            candidate = prices.get(key)
            if isinstance(candidate, dict):
                candidate = candidate.get("price") or candidate.get("current_price")
            parsed = _number(candidate)
            if parsed is not None and parsed > 0:
                return parsed
        return None

    @staticmethod
    def _move_from_snapshot(row: dict[str, Any], board: str) -> float | None:
        if not isinstance(row, dict):
            return None
        if board == "ignition_1m":
            keys = ("pct_1m", "change_1m", "price_change_percentage_1min")
        else:
            keys = ("pct_3m", "change_3m", "price_change_percentage_3min")
        for key in keys:
            value = _number(row.get(key))
            if value is not None:
                return value
        return None

    def _select_control(
        self,
        *,
        board: str,
        item: dict[str, Any],
        prices: dict[str, Any],
        excluded_products: set[str],
    ) -> dict[str, Any] | None:
        """Pick the closest same-direction non-board mover as a live control."""
        target_move = _number(item.get("change"))
        direction = str(item.get("direction") or "")
        if target_move is None or not direction:
            return None

        best: tuple[float, str, dict[str, Any]] | None = None
        for raw_key, raw_row in prices.items():
            symbol = _symbol(raw_key)
            if not symbol or symbol in _STABLECOINS or symbol == item.get("symbol"):
                continue
            product_id = str(raw_key or "").strip().upper()
            if "-" not in product_id:
                product_id = f"{symbol}-USD"
            if product_id in excluded_products:
                continue
            row = raw_row if isinstance(raw_row, dict) else {"price": raw_row}
            price = _number(
                row.get("price")
                if row.get("price") is not None
                else row.get("current_price")
            )
            move = self._move_from_snapshot(row, board)
            if price is None or price <= 0 or move is None or move == 0:
                continue
            if (direction == "up" and move <= 0) or (direction == "down" and move >= 0):
                continue
            distance = abs(abs(move) - abs(target_move))
            candidate = {
                "product_id": product_id,
                "symbol": symbol,
                "price": price,
                "change": move,
            }
            ordering = (distance, symbol, candidate)
            if best is None or ordering[:2] < best[:2]:
                best = ordering
        return dict(best[2]) if best else None

    def _cooldown_ready(
        self, conn: sqlite3.Connection, board: str, product_id: str, now_ts: int
    ) -> bool:
        previous = conn.execute(
            f"SELECT exited_ts FROM {TABLE_NAME} WHERE board = ? AND product_id = ? "
            "ORDER BY entered_ts DESC LIMIT 1",
            (board, product_id),
        ).fetchone()
        if previous is None:
            return True
        exited_ts = previous["exited_ts"]
        return (
            exited_ts is not None and (now_ts - int(exited_ts)) >= self.cooldown_seconds
        )

    def _direction_for(self, board: str, row: dict[str, Any]) -> str | None:
        if board == "confirmation_3m_up":
            return "up"
        if board == "confirmation_3m_down":
            return "down"
        change = _number(
            row.get("change_1m")
            if row.get("change_1m") is not None
            else row.get("price_change_percentage_1min")
        )
        if change is None or change == 0:
            return None
        return "up" if change > 0 else "down"

    def _classify_15m(
        self, directional_return: float, favorable: float, adverse: float
    ) -> str:
        threshold = self.move_threshold_pct
        if favorable >= threshold and adverse <= -threshold:
            return "volatile"
        if directional_return >= threshold:
            return "continuation"
        if directional_return <= -threshold:
            return "reversal"
        return "noise"

    def observe(
        self,
        boards: dict[str, list[dict[str, Any]] | None],
        prices: dict[str, Any],
        *,
        now_ts: int | None = None,
        ranking_snapshot: dict[str, dict[str, Any]] | None = None,
        ranking_captured_ts: int | None = None,
    ) -> None:
        """Observe current top-eight board membership and advance open outcomes.

        ``ranking_snapshot`` maps bare symbol -> a build_live_rankings() row
        built with include_internal_features=True.  ``ranking_captured_ts`` is
        the instant that ranking calculation started; both are required
        together, because a snapshot dated by this method's own clock would
        misdate the state it claims to describe.  Snapshots are written only on
        the INSERT for a new board entry, so an existing entry is never
        backfilled from a later cycle's evidence.
        """
        now_ts = int(now_ts or time.time())
        prices = prices if isinstance(prices, dict) else {}
        rankings = ranking_snapshot if isinstance(ranking_snapshot, dict) else {}
        if ranking_captured_ts is None:
            rankings = {}
        normalized: dict[str, dict[str, dict[str, Any]]] = {}

        for board in BOARD_NAMES:
            rows = boards.get(board)
            if rows is None:
                continue
            current: dict[str, dict[str, Any]] = {}
            for rank, row in enumerate((rows or [])[: self.visible_limit], start=1):
                if not isinstance(row, dict):
                    continue
                product_id, symbol = self._row_product(row)
                direction = self._direction_for(board, row)
                price = self._price_from_row(row, prices, product_id, symbol)
                if not product_id or not symbol or not direction or price is None:
                    continue
                change_key = "change_1m" if board == "ignition_1m" else "change_3m"
                change = _number(row.get(change_key))
                if change is None:
                    change = _number(
                        row.get("price_change_percentage_1min")
                        if board == "ignition_1m"
                        else row.get("price_change_percentage_3min")
                    )
                current[product_id] = {
                    "product_id": product_id,
                    "symbol": symbol,
                    "direction": direction,
                    "price": price,
                    "rank": rank,
                    "change": change,
                }
            normalized[board] = current

        excluded_products = {
            product_id for current in normalized.values() for product_id in current
        }

        conn = self._connect()
        try:
            # Measured before this cycle writes anything (see outcome_gaps).
            gap_start = self._gaps.gap_start(
                conn, now_ts, f"SELECT MAX(last_seen_ts) FROM {TABLE_NAME}"
            )
            with conn:
                for board, current in normalized.items():
                    active = {
                        row["product_id"]: row
                        for row in conn.execute(
                            f"SELECT id, product_id FROM {TABLE_NAME} WHERE board = ? AND exited_ts IS NULL",
                            (board,),
                        ).fetchall()
                    }
                    for product_id, row in active.items():
                        if product_id not in current:
                            conn.execute(
                                f"UPDATE {TABLE_NAME} SET exited_ts = ? WHERE id = ?",
                                (now_ts, row["id"]),
                            )
                    for product_id, item in current.items():
                        if product_id in active:
                            conn.execute(
                                f"UPDATE {TABLE_NAME} SET last_seen_ts = ? WHERE id = ?",
                                (now_ts, active[product_id]["id"]),
                            )
                            continue
                        if not self._cooldown_ready(conn, board, product_id, now_ts):
                            continue
                        control = self._select_control(
                            board=board,
                            item=item,
                            prices=prices,
                            excluded_products=excluded_products,
                        )
                        ranking_row = rankings.get(
                            str(item.get("symbol") or "").upper()
                        )
                        if ranking_row is not None:
                            feat_ver, feat_json = build_feature_snapshot(
                                ranking_row, int(ranking_captured_ts)
                            )
                        else:
                            feat_ver, feat_json = None, None
                        conn.execute(
                            f"""
                            INSERT INTO {TABLE_NAME} (
                                model_version,
                                board, product_id, symbol, direction, entered_ts,
                                last_seen_ts, start_price, entry_rank, start_change_pct,
                                control_product_id, control_start_price, control_change_pct,
                                estimated_cost_pct,
                                feature_schema_version, feature_snapshot_json
                            ) VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?)
                            """,
                            (
                                MODEL_VERSION,
                                board,
                                product_id,
                                item["symbol"],
                                item["direction"],
                                now_ts,
                                now_ts,
                                item["price"],
                                item["rank"],
                                item["change"],
                                control.get("product_id") if control else None,
                                control.get("price") if control else None,
                                control.get("change") if control else None,
                                self.round_trip_cost_pct,
                                feat_ver,
                                feat_json,
                            ),
                        )

                collecting = conn.execute(
                    f"SELECT * FROM {TABLE_NAME} WHERE complete = 0"
                ).fetchall()
                for row in collecting:
                    if open_across_gap(
                        int(row["entered_ts"]), self.horizon_seconds, gap_start
                    ):
                        # Checkpoints already measured on time stand; the rest
                        # of its hour wasn't watched, so it gets no 15m grade.
                        conn.execute(
                            f"UPDATE {TABLE_NAME} SET complete = 1, "
                            "outcome_15m = COALESCE(outcome_15m, ?) WHERE id = ?",
                            (INTERRUPTED, row["id"]),
                        )
                        continue
                    current = self._current_price(
                        prices, row["product_id"], row["symbol"]
                    )
                    if current is None:
                        continue
                    age = max(0, now_ts - int(row["entered_ts"]))
                    gross_directional = self._directional_return(
                        row["direction"], float(row["start_price"]), current
                    )
                    net_directional = gross_directional - float(
                        row["estimated_cost_pct"] or 0
                    )
                    control_directional = None
                    if row["control_product_id"] and row["control_start_price"]:
                        control_symbol = _symbol(row["control_product_id"])
                        control_current = self._current_price(
                            prices, row["control_product_id"], control_symbol
                        )
                        if control_current is not None:
                            control_directional = self._directional_return(
                                row["direction"],
                                float(row["control_start_price"]),
                                control_current,
                            ) - float(row["estimated_cost_pct"] or 0)
                    favorable = max(
                        float(row["max_favorable_pct"] or 0), gross_directional
                    )
                    adverse = min(float(row["max_adverse_pct"] or 0), gross_directional)
                    favorable_15m = float(row["max_favorable_15m"] or 0)
                    adverse_15m = float(row["max_adverse_15m"] or 0)
                    if age <= 900 or row["return_15m"] is None:
                        favorable_15m = max(favorable_15m, gross_directional)
                        adverse_15m = min(adverse_15m, gross_directional)

                    updates: dict[str, Any] = {
                        "max_favorable_pct": favorable,
                        "max_adverse_pct": adverse,
                        "max_favorable_15m": favorable_15m,
                        "max_adverse_15m": adverse_15m,
                    }
                    for seconds, column in CHECKPOINTS:
                        if age < seconds:
                            continue
                        if age - seconds > self._gaps.seconds:
                            continue  # missed; never filled in late
                        gross_column = f"gross_{column}"
                        control_column = f"control_{column}"
                        if row[gross_column] is None:
                            updates[gross_column] = gross_directional
                        if row[column] is None:
                            updates[column] = net_directional
                        if (
                            row[control_column] is None
                            and control_directional is not None
                        ):
                            updates[control_column] = control_directional
                    return_15m = updates.get("return_15m", row["return_15m"])
                    if return_15m is not None and row["outcome_15m"] is None:
                        updates["outcome_15m"] = self._classify_15m(
                            float(return_15m), favorable_15m, adverse_15m
                        )
                    if age >= self.horizon_seconds:
                        updates["complete"] = 1

                    assignments = ", ".join(f"{key} = ?" for key in updates)
                    conn.execute(
                        f"UPDATE {TABLE_NAME} SET {assignments} WHERE id = ?",
                        (*updates.values(), row["id"]),
                    )

                retention_days = max(
                    30, int(os.getenv("MW_BOARD_OUTCOME_RETENTION_DAYS", "180"))
                )
                conn.execute(
                    f"DELETE FROM {TABLE_NAME} WHERE complete = 1 AND entered_ts < ?",
                    (now_ts - retention_days * 86400,),
                )
                self._gaps.beat(conn, now_ts)
        finally:
            conn.close()
        # Outcome summaries are deliberately allowed to lag the scanner by at
        # most 30 seconds; re-querying thousands of historical rows every
        # eight-second price tick would add work without changing the read.

    def _summary_for(self, conn: sqlite3.Connection, board: str) -> dict[str, Any]:
        rows = conn.execute(
            f"""
            SELECT entered_ts, outcome_15m,
                   gross_return_5m, gross_return_15m, gross_return_30m, gross_return_60m,
                   return_5m, return_15m, return_30m, return_60m,
                   control_return_5m, control_return_15m, control_return_30m, control_return_60m
            FROM {TABLE_NAME}
            WHERE board = ? AND outcome_15m IS NOT NULL AND outcome_15m != 'interrupted'
            ORDER BY entered_ts DESC
            LIMIT 5000
            """,
            (board,),
        ).fetchall()
        counts = {name: 0 for name in ("continuation", "reversal", "volatile", "noise")}
        for row in rows:
            name = str(row["outcome_15m"] or "noise")
            counts[name if name in counts else "noise"] += 1
        total = len(rows)
        low, high = _wilson_interval(counts["continuation"], total)
        oldest_ts = min((int(row["entered_ts"]) for row in rows), default=None)
        newest_ts = max((int(row["entered_ts"]) for row in rows), default=None)
        span_seconds = (
            max(0, (newest_ts or 0) - (oldest_ts or 0)) if oldest_ts is not None else 0
        )
        span_days = span_seconds / 86400.0
        adequate = total >= self.minimum_samples and span_days >= self.minimum_span_days

        def median_for(column: str) -> float | None:
            values = [float(row[column]) for row in rows if row[column] is not None]
            return round(median(values), 3) if values else None

        def median_excess_for(column: str) -> float | None:
            control_column = f"control_{column}"
            values = [
                float(row[column]) - float(row[control_column])
                for row in rows
                if row[column] is not None and row[control_column] is not None
            ]
            return round(median(values), 3) if values else None

        matched_rows = [row for row in rows if row["control_return_15m"] is not None]
        control_counts = {name: 0 for name in ("continuation", "reversal", "noise")}
        for row in matched_rows:
            value = float(row["control_return_15m"])
            if value >= self.move_threshold_pct:
                control_counts["continuation"] += 1
            elif value <= -self.move_threshold_pct:
                control_counts["reversal"] += 1
            else:
                control_counts["noise"] += 1
        matched_total = len(matched_rows)
        continuation_rate = counts["continuation"] / total if total else None
        reversal_rate = counts["reversal"] / total if total else None
        control_continuation_rate = (
            control_counts["continuation"] / matched_total if matched_total else None
        )
        control_reversal_rate = (
            control_counts["reversal"] / matched_total if matched_total else None
        )
        continuation_lift = (
            continuation_rate - control_continuation_rate
            if continuation_rate is not None and control_continuation_rate is not None
            else None
        )
        reversal_lift = (
            reversal_rate - control_reversal_rate
            if reversal_rate is not None and control_reversal_rate is not None
            else None
        )
        excess_15m = median_excess_for("return_15m")

        if not adequate:
            read = "Learning"
        elif (
            continuation_lift is not None
            and continuation_lift >= 0.05
            and (excess_15m or 0) > 0
        ):
            read = "Continuation edge"
        elif reversal_lift is not None and reversal_lift >= 0.05:
            read = "Reversal tendency"
        else:
            read = "No clear edge"

        return {
            "board": board,
            "status": "measured" if adequate else "learning",
            "read": read,
            "sample_size": total,
            "matched_sample_size": matched_total,
            "minimum_samples": self.minimum_samples,
            "collection_span_days": round(span_days, 2),
            "minimum_span_days": self.minimum_span_days,
            "threshold_pct": self.move_threshold_pct,
            "estimated_round_trip_cost_pct": self.round_trip_cost_pct,
            "horizon_minutes": 15,
            "continuation_rate": (
                round(continuation_rate, 4) if continuation_rate is not None else None
            ),
            "reversal_rate": (
                round(reversal_rate, 4) if reversal_rate is not None else None
            ),
            "volatile_rate": round(counts["volatile"] / total, 4) if total else None,
            "noise_rate": round(counts["noise"] / total, 4) if total else None,
            "continuation_ci95": (
                [round(low, 4), round(high, 4)]
                if low is not None and high is not None
                else None
            ),
            "control": {
                "method": "nearest same-direction non-board mover",
                "sample_size": matched_total,
                "continuation_rate": (
                    round(control_continuation_rate, 4)
                    if control_continuation_rate is not None
                    else None
                ),
                "reversal_rate": (
                    round(control_reversal_rate, 4)
                    if control_reversal_rate is not None
                    else None
                ),
                "noise_rate": (
                    round(control_counts["noise"] / matched_total, 4)
                    if matched_total
                    else None
                ),
                "counts": control_counts,
            },
            "continuation_lift_vs_control": (
                round(continuation_lift, 4) if continuation_lift is not None else None
            ),
            "reversal_lift_vs_control": (
                round(reversal_lift, 4) if reversal_lift is not None else None
            ),
            "median_directional_return": {
                "5m": median_for("return_5m"),
                "15m": median_for("return_15m"),
                "30m": median_for("return_30m"),
                "60m": median_for("return_60m"),
            },
            "median_gross_directional_return": {
                "5m": median_for("gross_return_5m"),
                "15m": median_for("gross_return_15m"),
                "30m": median_for("gross_return_30m"),
                "60m": median_for("gross_return_60m"),
            },
            "median_excess_return_vs_control": {
                "5m": median_excess_for("return_5m"),
                "15m": excess_15m,
                "30m": median_excess_for("return_30m"),
                "60m": median_excess_for("return_60m"),
            },
            "counts": counts,
        }

    def status(self) -> dict[str, Any]:
        with self._summary_lock:
            if self._summary_cache and (time.monotonic() - self._summary_cache[0]) < 30:
                return dict(self._summary_cache[1])
        conn = self._connect()
        try:
            collecting = int(
                conn.execute(
                    f"SELECT COUNT(*) FROM {TABLE_NAME} WHERE complete = 0"
                ).fetchone()[0]
            )
            total = int(
                conn.execute(f"SELECT COUNT(*) FROM {TABLE_NAME}").fetchone()[0]
            )
            interrupted = int(
                conn.execute(
                    f"SELECT COUNT(*) FROM {TABLE_NAME} WHERE outcome_15m = ?",
                    (INTERRUPTED,),
                ).fetchone()[0]
            )
            boards = {board: self._summary_for(conn, board) for board in BOARD_NAMES}
            legacy_table = conn.execute(
